People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I suppose it's just some things at work. I teach secondary school, and... lately I've just been having a hard time with the staff emails. The other teachers try to gently show me that the emails were sent to everyone on staff, but... It just happens. And then it stops the whole conversation and completely interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing. It's... it's just very embarrassing for me. I'd rather not dwell on it, if that's alright.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense to you when you're not hearing the voice or worrying about the messages?\n\nPatient: Yes. Just... sometimes. \n\nWhen it's really quiet, like after the classroom clears out for the day, or when I'm alone at my house. I'll hear... a woman. She just says my name, or makes a quick comment about whatever I'm doing. It sounds so normal that I actually answer back... before I look around and realize there's no one else in the room. \n\nBut it's just short things. I don't really like to talk about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the woman's voice?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose it's been about four months. Give or take. \n\nThat's... that's around the time both things started, really. The voice, and... worrying about the messages. But like I said, it's just sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble organizing your thoughts when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I mean, I teach all day, so I have to be able to explain things clearly to the students. I haven't really noticed any... trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily routines since these things started?\n\nPatient: I... well, maybe a little. Just small things, really. \n\nSometimes at school, during my lessons or... the art-fair meetings... I'll catch myself just stopping right in the middle of a task. I'll start rearranging the markers on the desk, or my papers, into this very particular pattern. Or... if someone is talking to me, sometimes I just repeat a phrase they said instead of answering normally. \n\nIt's... well, it's embarrassing. It interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing. But I try to just move past it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the coded messages in the staff emails are actually about you, or is it just a feeling you can't shake?\n\nPatient: It's... well, I suppose it's a feeling I just can't seem to shake. \n\nMy colleagues... they've gently shown me that those emails go out to everyone on staff. I see that. But... when I read them, or when I hear the morning announcements... it just feels like they are actually coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the staff emails and morning announcements are actually talking about you in a hidden way, even when others tell you they're not?\n\nPatient: I... I believe I just answered that. Yes. Even when they show me the distribution list and I can see everyone received the exact same email... I still feel like there is a hidden meaning directed at me. That's... that's actually why I came in to see someone. Because I just can't make the feeling go away.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean when you say you repeat a phrase someone said instead of answering normally?\n\nPatient: I... well, I'm not sure what else to add to it, really. Like I mentioned... if another teacher asks me a question, or a student, I might just... say their exact words back to them instead of actually answering. \n\nI don't really mean to do it. It just happens. And then it stops the whole conversation and completely interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing. It's... it's just very embarrassing for me. I'd rather not dwell on it, if that's alright.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense to you when you're not hearing the voice or worrying about the messages?\n\nPatient: Yes. I... I believe they make perfect sense. I don't have any trouble thinking clearly, if that's what you're asking. My thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty performing your job as a teacher or experienced problems in your relationships with colleagues or students due to these feelings or experiences?\n\nPatient: I... suppose it has affected things a little. I am still teaching. But... well, some of my coworkers have had to step in lately. Just to cover parts of my project lessons, or... help me actually get through the planning meetings. \n\nAnd outside of work... I guess I've just stopped being reliable. For my book club, or the theater group... neighborhood events. I just... haven't been keeping up with any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your repeating of phrases or rearranging items is related to the voices or the coded messages you believe you're receiving?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I haven't really thought about it like that. I just... do it. I'm not sure if they're connected.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble finding the right words when speaking or feel like your thoughts are disjointed?\n\nPatient: I... no. I don't think so. I usually know what I want to say. I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I'll start rearranging the markers on the desk, or my papers, into this very particular pattern. Or... if someone is talking to me, sometimes I just repeat a phrase they said instead of answering normally. \n\nIt's... well, it's embarrassing. It interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing. But I try to just move past it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the coded messages in the staff emails are actually about you, or is it just a feeling you can't shake?\n\nPatient: It's... well, I suppose it's a feeling I just can't seem to shake. \n\nMy colleagues... they've gently shown me that those emails go out to everyone on staff. I see that. But... when I read them, or when I hear the morning announcements... it just feels like they are actually coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the staff emails and morning announcements are actually talking about you in a hidden way, even when others tell you they're not?\n\nPatient: I... I believe I just answered that. Yes. Even when they show me the distribution list and I can see everyone received the exact same email... I still feel like there is a hidden meaning directed at me. That's... that's actually why I came in to see someone. Because I just can't make the feeling go away.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean when you say you repeat a phrase someone said instead of answering normally?\n\nPatient: I... well, I'm not sure what else to add to it, really. Like I mentioned... if another teacher asks me a question, or a student, I might just... say their exact words back to them instead of actually answering. \n\nI don't really mean to do it. It just happens. And then it stops the whole conversation and completely interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing. It's... it's just very embarrassing for me. I'd rather not dwell on it, if that's alright.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense to you when you're not hearing the voice or worrying about the messages?\n\nPatient: Yes. I... I believe they make perfect sense. I don't have any trouble thinking clearly, if that's what you're asking. My thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty performing your job as a teacher or experienced problems in your relationships with colleagues or students due to these feelings or experiences?\n\nPatient: I... suppose it has affected things a little. I am still teaching. But... well, some of my coworkers have had to step in lately. Just to cover parts of my project lessons, or... help me actually get through the planning meetings. \n\nAnd outside of work... I guess I've just stopped being reliable. For my book club, or the theater group... neighborhood events. I just... haven't been keeping up with any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your repeating of phrases or rearranging items is related to the voices or the coded messages you believe you're receiving?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I haven't really thought about it like that. I just... do it. I'm not sure if they're connected.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble finding the right words when speaking or feel like your thoughts are disjointed?\n\nPatient: I... no. I don't think so. I usually know what I want to say. I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt anxious, depressed, or overwhelmed by these experiences and beliefs about coded messages and the voice?\n\nPatient: I... not really. I mean, it's just... what's happening. I wouldn't say I feel depressed or anything like that. I just... try to get through the day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't really like to talk about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the woman's voice?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose it's been about four months. She just says my name, or makes a quick comment about whatever I'm doing. It sounds so normal that I actually answer back... before I look around and realize there's no one else in the room. \n\nBut it's just short things. I don't really like to talk about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the woman's voice?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose it's been about four months. Give or take. \n\nThat's... that's around the time both things started, really. The voice, and... worrying about the messages. But like I said, it's just sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble organizing your thoughts when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I mean, I teach all day, so I have to be able to explain things clearly to the students. I haven't really noticed any... trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily routines since these things started?\n\nPatient: I... well, maybe a little. Just small things, really. \n\nSometimes at school, during my lessons or... the art-fair meetings... I'll catch myself just stopping right in the middle of a task. I'll start rearranging the markers on the desk, or my papers, into this very particular pattern. Or... if someone is talking to me, sometimes I just repeat a phrase they said instead of answering normally. \n\nIt's... well, it's embarrassing. It interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing. But I try to just move past it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the coded messages in the staff emails are actually about you, or is it just a feeling you can't shake?\n\nPatient: It's... well, I suppose it's a feeling I just can't seem to shake. \n\nMy colleagues... they've gently shown me that those emails go out to everyone on staff. I see that. But... when I read them, or when I hear the morning announcements... it just feels like they are actually coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the staff emails and morning announcements are actually talking about you in a hidden way, even when others tell you they're not?\n\nPatient: I... I believe I just answered that. Yes. Even when they show me the distribution list and I can see everyone received the exact same email... I still feel like there is a hidden meaning directed at me. That's... that's actually why I came in to see someone. Because I just can't make the feeling go away.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean when you say you repeat a phrase someone said instead of answering normally?\n\nPatient: I... well, I'm not sure what else to add to it, really. Like I mentioned... if another teacher asks me a question, or a student, I might just... say their exact words back to them instead of actually answering. \n\nI don't really mean to do it. It just happens. And then it stops the whole conversation and completely interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing. It's... it's just very embarrassing for me. I'd rather not dwell on it, if that's alright.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense to you when you're not hearing the voice or worrying about the messages?\n\nPatient: Yes. I... I believe they make perfect sense. I don't have any trouble thinking clearly, if that's what you're asking. My thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty performing your job as a teacher or experienced problems in your relationships with colleagues or students due to these feelings or experiences?\n\nPatient: I... suppose it has affected things a little. I am still teaching. But... well, some of my coworkers have had to step in lately. Just to cover parts of my project lessons, or... help me actually get through the planning meetings. \n\nAnd outside of work... I guess I've just stopped being reliable. For my book club, or the theater group... neighborhood events. I just... haven't been keeping up with any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your repeating of phrases or rearranging items is related to the voices or the coded messages you believe you're receiving?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. I haven't really thought about it like that. I just... do it. I'm not sure if they're connected.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble finding the right words when speaking or feel like your thoughts are disjointed?\n\nPatient: I... no. I don't think so. I usually know what I want to say. I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt anxious, depressed, or overwhelmed by these experiences and beliefs about coded messages and the voice?\n\nPatient: I... not really. I mean, it's just... what's happening. I wouldn't say I feel depressed or anything like that. I just... try to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the coded messages in the staff emails and morning announcements are unique to you, or could they potentially be about someone else?\n\nPatient: I... well, I know my colleagues say they're sent to everyone. They've... gently pointed that out to me. But... I just can't shake the feeling that they're about me. The emails, the announcements... it just feels like they're meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the messages in the emails and announcements are about you because of something specific you've done or something about you in particular?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure. I haven't done anything wrong, if that's what you mean. It's just... a feeling I get. I don't really know why it's happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your life or daily activities have been significantly impacted by these beliefs about coded messages and the voice?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm still teaching. It's not like I've stopped working. But... I suppose things are a little harder. I've needed some of the other teachers to... cover parts of my project lessons lately. And just to help me get through our planning meetings. \n\nOutside of work... I don't know. I guess I've stopped being very reliable. I used to go to a book club, and the theater, and... neighborhood events. But I just haven't been keeping up with those.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant change in your ability to perform daily tasks or maintain relationships since these experiences started?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure what you consider significant. I... I still do what I need to do every day. I run my errands, keep up with the house. As for people... I don't know. We just haven't spoken much lately. That's all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

She just says my name, or makes a quick comment about whatever I'm doing. It sounds so normal that I actually answer back... before I look around and realize there's no one else in the room. \n\nBut it's just short things.
